  - [reference-only] CodeRabbit https://www.coderabbit.ai/ :: STEAL: Know exactly what you are being compared against — it is no longer light :: SPECIFICS: body lab(4.9349 1.26021 -1.99696) = #111014. Geist 500, h1 64px / 68px, letter-spacing -3%, with a per-character animated verb swap ('reviewing it.' -> 'securing it.' -> 'prioritiz...'). 2 canvases, 0 videos. Grain: url("data:image/svg+xml,...viewBox='0 0 180 180'...feTurbulence type='fractalNoise' baseFrequency='1.16' numOctaves='4' stitchTiles='stitch'...") used as background AND mask-image. Radix 'mauve' scale + a custom --color-cui-cr-orange-9 accent. Easings cubic-bezier(.23,1,.32,1) x11 and cubic-bezier(.22,1,.36,1) x8. Section h2s: 'Fast code. Faster chaos.' / 'Raise the quality bar. Lower the review burden.'
  - [avoid] Qodo https://www.qodo.ai/ :: STEAL: Nothing visual — steal the warning. They own your intended headline word. :: SPECIFICS: body rgb(17,16,19) = #111013. h1 Figtree 500, 72px / 73.44px, letter-spacing -1.44px: 'Code review for agents. Governance for humans.' No canvas, no video, 0 mix-blend elements — a visually flat dark site whose whole advantage is the copy. PRGuard must out-position, not out-render, this one.
  - [strong] cubic.dev https://cubic.dev/ :: STEAL: The 'ultralight + positive tracking + centre spotlight' counter-move to everyone else's tight bold type :: SPECIFICS: body #060707 (the darkest in the category). h1 Geist weight 200 (!), 68px / 81.6px (lh 1.2), letter-spacing +2.04px (+0.03em — POSITIVE, against the whole trend). Centred single-column hero, one small metallic 3D mark under a top radial spotlight, white pill primary + ghost secondary, Product Hunt badge, and the line 'Used by teams that can't afford bugs.' Uses Lottie.
  - [reference-only] Greptile https://www.greptile.com/ :: STEAL: Proof that the light-editorial counter-position exists in this exact category; also the metric-in-title-tag tactic :: SPECIFICS: body #E9E9E9, ink #3D3B4F. h1 = Anybody (variable-width, SIL OFL via Google Fonts) weight 700, 96px / 96px, letter-spacing -2.4px. Body DM Sans, code Geist Mono, plus Nanum Pen Script for handwritten annotations. Emerald grid animation pulses --grid-color between #10b9810d and #10b9811f. Still on Tailwind v3 (--tw-* vars, zero oklch, zero color-mix). Title tag: 'Merge 4X Faster, Catch 3X More Bugs'.
  - [must-steal] Cerebrium — Awwwards SOTD 10 Sep 2026 https://cerebrium.ai/ :: STEAL: The one legitimately cinematic AI-infra site: a single full-viewport WebGL ribbon behind DOM type :: SPECIFICS: html background rgb(16,20,33) = #101421 (blue-black, NOT neutral). ONE canvas at 2880x1800 (= DPR2 of 1440x900) — full-viewport, everything else is DOM. h1 'ABC Favorit' Light 300 at 86.09px / 86.09px line-height, ls -2.15px (-0.025em); body Suisse Int'l. Pink accent #FF488B. Ships a 'c-initial-loader'. Stack: Lenis + Motion + Lottie. WARNING: they are serving 'ABC Favorit Trial' in production — a licence violation; do not copy that part.
  - [strong] illoca by Unseen Studio — Awwwards SOTD 4 Sep 2026 https://illoca.unseen.co/ :: STEAL: The current award formula in its purest form: one canvas, giant sub-1.0 leading, tiny JS :: SPECIFICS: h1 'F37 Analog' 500 at 111px with line-height 99.9px (0.90 leading — tighter than the type size). ONE canvas at 2880x1800. Single Vite chunk (DGk6hhCZ.js). Lenis only. Has a 'loader' element. The h1 node contains the headline three times over (scroll-swapped variants), a cheap way to get a kinetic hero without a text-splitting library.
  - [must-steal] Railway https://railway.com/ :: STEAL: Serif headline + purple-black base — the warmest, least generic dark in dev infra :: SPECIFICS: body rgb(19,17,28) = #13111C (violet-black, not neutral). h1 IBM Plex Serif 500, 54px / 60.48px, letter-spacing -1.96px (-0.036em): 'Ship software peacefully'. Body Inter. 8 mix-blend elements, 0 canvas, 0 video. IBM Plex is SIL OFL-1.1 (verified: github.com/IBM/plex), so this exact look is free to reproduce.
  - [must-steal] Modal https://modal.com/ :: STEAL: Semantic token naming that maps 1:1 onto PRGuard's severity/proof states, and tinted-white type :: SPECIFICS: body #000, h1 'Goga' 500 at 64px/64px in rgb(221,255,220) = #DDFFDC (mint-tinted white, not #FFF). 8 videos, 2 canvases. Token system: --semantic-background-{accent,primary,secondary,success,warning,caution,danger,running,informational} each with -hover and -active variants. 'running' as a first-class semantic colour is exactly what a 9-stage pipeline UI needs. Fonts Degular / degular-display / ABC Diatype / DM Mono (all paid).
  - [strong] e2b.dev https://e2b.dev/ :: STEAL: Syntax-highlight colours promoted to first-class brand tokens — perfect for a proof-terminal product :: SPECIFICS: --color-bg #000. --color-code-keyword = --color-code-orange-dark #ff795b; --color-code-string = --color-code-green-dark #9dc20c; --color-code-function = --color-code-purple-dark #b57cff; --color-code-blue-dark #5696fd. Easing cubic-bezier(.32,.72,0,1) x6 (same as Linear's) and cubic-bezier(.23,1,.32,1) x3. Fonts: Neue Haas Grotesk Text Pro + PP Supply Sans + Commit Mono (Commit Mono is SIL OFL-1.1 and free). Lenis.
  - [strong] basement.studio https://basement.studio/ :: STEAL: Sub-1.0 leading on a very large Geist headline — the cheapest 'expensive' typographic move there is :: SPECIFICS: body #000. h1 Geist 600 at 87px with line-height 78px = 0.897 leading, letter-spacing -3.48px (-0.04em). Accent orange rgb(255,77,0) = #FF4D00. Geist + Geist Mono + a custom 'flauta'. Uses GSAP. 3 unsized canvases. Only one easing in their CSS: cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1) x14 — proof that the craft is in the type and the render pipeline, not the easing.
  - [strong] Cursor https://cursor.com/ :: STEAL: WARM near-black. The single fastest way to not look like every other dev tool. :: SPECIFICS: body rgb(20,18,11) = #14120B — an olive/warm black, measurably off-neutral. Bespoke 'CursorGothic' sans and a --font-serif stack falling back to Iowan Old Style / Palatino. Palette hexes in page: #EDECEC, #CAC6BE, #47433C, #3C3935, #26251E, #C08532, #C9A227, #14120B. Serves logo-dark-theme.mp4. Zero canvases.
  - [strong] Dimension https://www.dimension.dev/ :: STEAL: How far you can get on 8 MP4s and mix-blend-mode with no 3D at all :: SPECIFICS: body rgb(12,12,12) = #0C0C0C. h1 Geist 500 at 48px/48px, letter-spacing normal (notably NOT tightened). Body DM Sans. 8 videos, 0 canvases, 17 elements with non-normal mix-blend-mode (screen, plus-lighter, color-burn all present), 18 backdrop-filter rules. Easings include cubic-bezier(.08,.82,.17,1) and cubic-bezier(.33,1,.68,1).
  - [strong] boc.studio — Awwwards SOTD 19 Sep 2026 https://boc.studio/ :: STEAL: A WIDE STRIP canvas instead of a full-page one — 10% of the GPU cost, most of the effect :: SPECIFICS: body rgb(24,29,33) = #181D21 (cool slate-black). ONE canvas at 2880x346 — a horizontal band, not a viewport fill. PP Mori type. Stack: Lenis + Motion + Rive (no GSAP, no Three). One video. No preloader.
  - [reference-only] Oxide Computer https://oxide.computer/ :: STEAL: The most 'industrial instrument' dark editorial voice in tech, and a genuinely mechanical easing :: SPECIFICS: Suisse Int'l + GT America Mono (both paid). Signature easing cubic-bezier(.87,0,.13,1) x4 — a hard ease-in-out-quart that reads as a machine actuating, not a UI bouncing. Also cubic-bezier(.95,.05,.795,.035) and cubic-bezier(.895,.03,.685,.22). --surface-raise #0000001a.
  - [must-steal] Paper Shaders https://shaders.paper.design/ :: STEAL: The whole cinematic layer, in one Apache-2.0 dependency :: SPECIFICS: npm i @paper-design/shaders-react — v0.0.81, published 2026-09-17, Apache-2.0, 3,471 stars, zero runtime dependencies, canvas (not Three.js). Components: GodRays, GrainGradient, MeshGradient, StaticMeshGradient, StaticRadialGradient, Dithering, ImageDithering, HalftoneDots, HalftoneCmyk, PulsingBorder, Metaballs, LiquidMetal, GemSmoke, Heatmap, SmokeRing, Voronoi, NeuroNoise, PerlinNoise, SimplexNoise, Warp, Spiral, Swirl, Waves, ColorPanels, DotOrbit, DotGrid, FlutedGlass, LensDistortion, Water, PaperTexture.
RECOMMENDATIONS:
  - (high) Do NOT lead with the word 'governance'. Qodo's live h1 is literally 'Code review for agents. Governance for humans.' Lead with PROOF as the single hero noun — e.g. 'Every claim, proven. At the base SHA and the head SHA.' Governance becomes section 4 (the audit chain), not the headline. | WHY: Measured on qodo.ai 2026-09-22. Landing on a competitor's exact headline noun in a hackathon judged on differentiation is fatal, and 'proof by execution' is the one mechanic no competitor in the set claims. CodeRabbit sells volume ('reviewing/securing/prioritizing it'), Graphite sells 'next generation', Greptile sells speed ('Merge 4X Faster'), cubic sells 'complex codebases', Qodo sells governance. Proof is open ground. | TRADEOFF: 'Proof' is a bigger promise and invites the judge to ask 'show me a disproven finding'. You must have the disproven demo ready or the positioning backfires.
  - (high) Make the signature moment a SPLIT-SCREEN VERDICT, not a 3D object. Left: 'Tool A: this MIGHT null-deref' rendered as a grey, quoted, low-contrast card. Right: PRGuard's PROVEN panel with the generated repro test, the real terminal output at base SHA (pass, green) and head SHA (fail, red), and the stack trace — with the two runs revealed on a pinned scroll in three beats. Build it with Motion + a pinned sticky section, not GSAP ScrollTrigger. | WHY: Awwwards weights Design 40 / Usability 30 / Creativity 20 / Content 10 — Usability+Content equals Design, so a legible, high-content moment is not handicapped. And empirically the current winner stack is Lenis+Motion+Rive (13/9/7 of 21) with GSAP at 3/21; Motion 13.4.1 is MIT and you are already in React. The demo line 'We say it DOES — here's the failing test' only lands if the terminal output is real text you can select, not a video. | TRADEOFF: A DOM-based split screen will never out-spectacle a Three.js hero on pure wow. You are trading 2 seconds of gasp for 20 seconds of belief. For a product hackathon that is the right trade; for a pure design award it is not.
  - (high) Pick a BLUE-BLACK base with a cyan/amber proof axis, and ban purple. Base oklch(0.155 0.014 255) ≈ #0A0D14; elevation ladder #0A0D14 / #101520 / #161C2A / #1E2536 (borrow Raycast's four-step structure, shifted cold). Borders: #ffffff14 hairline, #ffffff25 for the focused ring (Vercel's value). PROVEN = #7AEBFF with a #7AEBFF33 glow (USAvionix's exact pair). DISPROVEN/self-refuted = #FFCB47. BLOCKING = #FF6666, used nowhere else on the site. | WHY: The category has converged on neutral near-black (#0A0A0A Graphite, #060707 cubic, #111014 CodeRabbit, #111013 Qodo, #000 Resend/Modal/basement) and on purple accents (#8B8BF5 CodeAnt, #7267ff Warp, #8E64FF Evervault). A cold blue-black with an instrument-cyan accent reads as an oscilloscope/flight-deck, which is exactly the 'blast radius / gate / audit' story, and is measurably unoccupied in this competitive set. | TRADEOFF: Cyan-on-black is the most common 'cyberpunk hackathon' cliché if you over-saturate it. It only reads expensive at low coverage: accent should be under ~4% of hero pixels, glow at 20% alpha, everything else greyscale.
  - (high) Do not set the h1 in Geist or Inter. Set it in IBM Plex Serif 500 or IBM Plex Mono 500 (both SIL OFL-1.1, free), at 64–80px with line-height 0.95 and letter-spacing -0.035em. Keep Inter/Geist for body and Commit Mono (OFL) or JetBrains Mono (OFL) for all data, hashes, SHAs and terminal output. | WHY: Geist is the category uniform (CodeRabbit, cubic, basement.studio, Dimension, USAvionix, trigger.dev all measured using it). Every site that reads expensive breaks the body font in the headline: Warp uses Matter Mono, Railway uses IBM Plex Serif, Resend uses Domaine, Modal uses Goga, Cursor uses CursorGothic. IBM Plex Serif gets you Railway's exact register for free — verified OFL-1.1 at github.com/IBM/plex. A mono h1 also literally means 'this is a machine that executed something'. | TRADEOFF: Serif/mono headlines at 64px+ need real hierarchy work or they look like a manifesto. Mono at display size costs horizontal space — cap the headline at ~6 words. Avoid Berkeley Mono/TX-02: it is paid and I could not verify current pricing.
  - (medium) Buy the whole cinematic layer with @paper-design/shaders-react (Apache-2.0, v0.0.81) instead of Three.js. Use exactly three: <GodRays> as a single low-opacity hero backdrop, <GrainGradient> for the ambient field, <PulsingBorder> on the live gate/check-run widget. Cap it at ONE full-bleed shader surface on the page. | WHY: Zero-dependency canvas shaders, no scene graph, no GLTF pipeline, no draco decoder, no camera rig — hours not days. The award set confirms the shape: winners run ONE canvas (illoca 2880x1800, Cerebrium 2880x1800) or a strip (boc.studio 2880x346), with everything else DOM. Three.js is 0.186.0 and fine, but @react-three/fiber 9.7.0 + drei 10.7.8 + a model is a two-day detour you do not have. | TRADEOFF: v0.0.81 is pre-1.0 — the API can break and there is little documentation. Pin the exact version and vendor a fallback: a static PNG/CSS gradient behind a feature check, so a shader failure degrades to a still frame rather than a white box.
  - (high) Fake all product 3D with pre-rendered, muted, looping MP4/WebM. Render the 9-stage pipeline 'machine', the sandbox container and the hash-chain link in Blender at 1600x1000, export ~6s loops under 1.5 MB each, and serve them with poster frames. Zero WebGL for product visuals. | WHY: Directly measured: Resend serves cube.mp4, 3d-broadcast.mp4, 3d-react.mp4, 3d-control.mp4 with ZERO canvases; Modal has 8 videos + 2 canvases; Dimension has 8 videos + 0 canvases; Linear has 0 of either above the fold. The three most 'expensive-feeling' dark product sites in the category do not do real-time 3D at all. An Awwwards juror quoted on this explicitly warns that 'a 3D hero that drops to 18fps on a mid-range Android... will not win'. | TRADEOFF: Video is not interactive and cannot respond to the viewer, and 4 loops at 1.5 MB is 6 MB of payload — lazy-load everything below the fold and preload only the hero. Also: only 8% of 2026 winners use video backgrounds (down from 40%+ in 2022), so full-bleed video hero specifically reads dated; use contained device-frame video instead.
  - (high) Ship the exact Linear edge system on every card in both the marketing site and the dashboard: box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px #ffffff08, inset 0 1px #ffffff0a, 0 0 0 1px #0009; plus radial-gradient(50% 50%, #ffffff0a 0%, #fff0 90%) as the panel fill; plus a pointer-tracked radial-gradient border driven by --edge-highlight-x/--edge-highlight-y written from a single rAF-throttled mousemove listener. | WHY: This is the highest craft-per-line-of-CSS in the entire survey. White at 3% (0x08) with a 4% top highlight (0x0a) is what makes a dark card look milled rather than filled. It costs no JS budget, no dependency, no asset, and works identically in the dashboard. Pair it with Vercel's #ffffff25 for the focus ring so keyboard focus is visibly brighter than rest state. | TRADEOFF: The pointer-tracked glow needs a rAF throttle and a prefers-reduced-motion / coarse-pointer bail-out, or it will jank on touch and burn battery. Do not apply it to more than ~8 cards on screen at once.
  - (medium) Use a preloader — but exactly one, capped at 900ms, that IS the product claim: a hash-chain verifying itself. Show 'verifying audit chain… seq 1..N' with the hashes resolving and ending on 'chain.verified = true', then cross-fade out. Skip it entirely on repeat visits via sessionStorage, and never let it block first paint beyond 900ms. | WHY: 8 of 21 current Awwwards winners ship a loader (38%), including why.zero.university's literally-named 'webgl-loader-overlay' and era-residence's theme-aware 'master-preloader theme_on-dark'. Preloaders are not dead, but they survive only where they do a job. A hash-chain verification loader is the rare case where the loader is itself the demo of M4 — it earns its 900ms. | TRADEOFF: Any loader costs you LCP and risks an impatient judge. Hard-cap it, make it skippable on click, and make sure the page is fully interactive underneath so the bail-out is instant.
  - (high) Grain: one fixed, pointer-events:none overlay using an inline data-URI SVG — feTurbulence type='fractalNoise' baseFrequency='0.9' numOctaves='4' stitchTiles='stitch' on a 180x180 tile — at 3–4% opacity, applied to the body, never to individual cards. Do NOT animate it. | WHY: This is CodeRabbit's own technique (baseFrequency 1.16, numOctaves 4, 180x180, stitchTiles) and USAvionix's (baseFrequency 0.28, numOctaves 1, plus feColorMatrix saturate 0). Linear instead uses a static PNG tile — either is fine; the data URI avoids a request. Grain is what stops a flat #0A0D14 from looking like an unstyled div, and it costs ~0.4 KB. | TRADEOFF: feTurbulence is rasterised once but is expensive on very large surfaces and on some Android GPUs; keep the tile at 180x180 and let it repeat. Above ~6% opacity it starts to fight text contrast and reads as a cheap filter.
  - (high) Standardise on exactly two easings and publish them as tokens: --ease-out: cubic-bezier(.32,.72,0,1) for anything entering or settling (Linear's and e2b's most-used), and --ease-emphasis: cubic-bezier(.16,1,.3,1) for the signature-moment reveals (Warp's and Raycast's). Never use Tailwind's default ease-in-out (.4,0,.2,1) on anything a judge will look at. | WHY: Measured frequencies across the survey: Linear 7x on (.32,.72,0,1); Raycast 12x on (.23,1,.32,1) and 8x on (.16,1,.3,1); Warp 8x on (.16,1,.3,1); CodeRabbit 11x on (.23,1,.32,1). Meanwhile basement.studio uses (.4,0,.2,1) 14x and Dimension 60x — proof that the default is where craft goes to die when the rest of the site isn't carrying it. Two easings consistently applied read as a system; six read as accidents. | TRADEOFF: None material. Durations matter as much: 180–260ms for UI state, 600–900ms for section reveals, 1.2s for the displacement/materialise moment.
  - (medium) For the M5 severity visual, do NOT ship a radar chart. Ship a horizontal stacked contribution bar: six segments sized by weight×input (reachability .20, blast_radius .15, path_sensitivity .25, coverage_gap .15, churn .10, proof_confidence .15), with the running total and band threshold markers at .15/.35/.55/.75 drawn on the same axis, and the sentence rendered underneath in mono. | WHY: A radar chart with six axes is unreadable at a glance and is the single most over-used 'AI scoring' visual. A weighted stacked bar shows contribution, total AND the threshold it crossed in one horizontal read — the judge sees 'why it is critical' in under a second. Modal's --semantic-background-{success,warning,caution,danger,running} token naming is the right substrate for the band colours. | TRADEOFF: You lose the shape-recognition of a radar (two findings with different profiles look similar). Mitigate by ordering segments consistently and colouring path_sensitivity and proof_confidence distinctly, since those carry the story.
  - (high) Dashboard stack: @tanstack/react-table 9.2.4 for findings/suppressions/audit, cmdk 1.1.1 for the keyboard palette, shiki 4.4.3 for diffs and terminal output (real tokens, not a screenshot), @number-flow/react 0.6.2 for live counters during a run, sonner 2.0.8 for stage transitions, nuqs 2.10.1 to put every filter in the URL, @xyflow/react 12.11.6 for the 9-stage pipeline graph. Same tokens as the marketing site, no separate theme. | WHY: All MIT, all current as of 2026-09-22, all zero-config with Next 16.3.6 + Tailwind 4.3.3. nuqs matters specifically for a demo: every filtered view becomes a shareable URL a judge can be dropped into. shiki matters because the proof terminal output must be selectable text — that is the whole credibility play. | TRADEOFF: xyflow is ~70 KB gzip for what could be a static SVG; only pull it in if the pipeline graph is genuinely interactive (click a stage -> see its logs). Otherwise hand-author the 9-stage SVG.
  - (high) Use the View Transitions API for marketing->dashboard and finding-list->finding-detail navigation, behind a `if (document.startViewTransition)` guard. Do NOT build the 9-stage scroll on CSS animation-timeline. | WHY: Verified against the web-features explorer: View Transitions hit Baseline 'Newly Available' on 2025-10-14 (Chrome 111+, Safari 18+, Firefox 144+) and are used on only ~1.34% of page loads — so it degrades safely and still surprises. CSS scroll-driven animations are NOT Baseline: Firefox has blocked availability since September 2025 and does not support it in stable; Chrome 115+ and Safari 26+ only. Scroll choreography must run on Motion/Lenis. | TRADEOFF: View transitions on a heavy dark page can flash if the two states have different backgrounds — set an explicit background on ::view-transition-old/new and keep the transition under 300ms.
  - (high) Build no custom cursor and no sound. Not a magnetic cursor, not a dot-follower, not a hover chime. | WHY: Hard count: 2 of 21 current Awwwards SOTD winners have a custom-cursor element (~10%), and 0 of 21 have any <audio>, .mp3, Howler or Tone.js reference. Both are 2022–2024 signatures that now read as a portfolio site cosplaying as a product. A B2B tool that hijacks the pointer also actively loses Usability points, which are worth 30% of the Awwwards score. | TRADEOFF: You give up one easy 'craft' signal. Spend that budget on the edge-bevel system and the proof moment instead — both survive scrutiny, a cursor trail does not.
  - (high) Copy Warp's habit of putting a NUMBER in every section h2. Warp's live h2s include 'Cutting our cost per PR from $80 to $30'. PRGuard's equivalents: 'Stage 3/9 — specialists, 12s since last output', 'proven at 7e3f1a, failed at 9c2d4b', 'execution-verified precision: 0.91 (n=312)', 'chain.verified = true, 1,482 events, broken_at_seq = null'. | WHY: Content is 10% of the Awwwards score and 100% of hackathon-judge credibility. The whole PRGuard thesis is that it ships its inputs, its n and its caveats — the site should behave the same way. Numbers in headings are also the cheapest possible differentiator against a category whose h2s are 'Catch them all', 'Fast code. Faster chaos.', 'The next generation of code review.' | TRADEOFF: Every number you publish must be defensible live. Show the definition and the n inline (your /v1/accuracy endpoint already carries both) or a sharp judge will call it marketing.
ANTI-PATTERNS / AVOID:
  - A neutral #0A0A0A or pure #000 base with a purple accent and Geist at every size. This is now the literal uniform of the AI-code-review category — Graphite is #0A0A0A, cubic #060707, CodeRabbit #111014, Qodo #111013, Resend/Modal/basement.studio/USAvionix all #000; CodeAnt's h1 is #8B8BF5, Warp's accent is #7267ff, Evervault's is #8E64FF. Shipping this is shipping a competitor's site with your logo on it.
  - Pure #FFFFFF type on pure #000000. Every site that reads expensive tints the foreground: Modal #DDFFDC, Linear #F7F8F8, Graphite #FAFAFA, Vercel #EDEDED, Raycast #c2c7ca. Max-contrast white on black is the fastest visual tell of a template or an LLM-generated page.
  - Tailwind's default `ease-in-out` / cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1) on hero and section animations. It is the transition curve of an un-art-directed site. Raycast uses (.23,1,.32,1) 12 times; Linear uses (.32,.72,0,1) 7 times; Warp uses (.16,1,.3,1) 8 times. Judges cannot name the curve but they can feel the difference.
  - Custom cursors of any kind — dot followers, magnetic buttons, trailing rings, flashlight reveals. 2 of 21 current Awwwards winners have one. On a B2B governance tool it actively costs Usability points (30% of the Awwwards score) and signals 'portfolio site' rather than 'product'.
  - Site audio: hover chimes, click clicks, ambient beds, or an 'enter with sound / without sound' gate. Zero of 21 current winners have any audio at all. This is a fully dead 2022–2023 signature.
  - Aurora / mesh / 'AI purple-to-cyan' blurred blobs behind the hero. Creatives polled for 2026 explicitly list gradients — specifically the golden-hued defaults that AI tools emit — as a trend they are over. A blurred radial blob is now read as 'no art direction happened here'.
  - Glassmorphism and Apple Liquid Glass imitation. It is the third item on the 2026 'creatives are so over it' list ('Liquid glass', 'Glass everything'), Apple itself had to rework it for readability in June 2026, and it costs real contrast on a dark data-dense dashboard. Surviving use is narrow: a translucent sticky header (Linear's backdrop-filter: saturate(1.8) blur(20px)) and modal scrims. Nothing else.
  - Bento grids as the default 'features' section. Named on the 2026 over-it list, admitted to even by people still using them. Four glowing rounded rectangles in a 2x2 with a gradient border is the most-copied section on the internet.
  - A full-bleed autoplaying video background behind the hero headline. Reportedly down to ~8% of 2026 winners from 40%+ in 2022. Contained device-frame video is fine; full-bleed reads 2019.
  - A Three.js hero with an orbiting abstract object (torus knot, particle sphere, floating glass cube, rotating logo). Two costs: it is the most predictable 'we tried' move, and a juror quoted on 2026 judging says a 3D hero that drops to 18fps on a mid-range Android will not win. If you cannot hold 60fps on a throttled phone, do not ship it.
  - Scroll-jacking: a full-page scroll hijack where one wheel tick advances one 'slide'. Smooth scroll (Lenis, 13/21 winners) is not scroll-jacking. Locking the viewport for 9 stages will get you closed.
  - Marquee logo ribbons of customer logos on a hackathon product that has no customers. Also fake testimonial cards, fake ARR counters, fake '17K customers' style metrics. In a product whose entire pitch is 'we prove our claims', a fabricated social-proof row is a self-inflicted credibility wound the judges will find.
  - Kinetic typography on more than the single hero line — letters stretching, snapping, splitting and recombining through every section. Described by 2026 commentary as 'more polish than substance'. One kinetic moment is a signature; four is a screensaver.
  - Y2K / retro-OS / pixel-window chrome / Windows-95 nostalgia. On the 2026 over-it list ('uniformed Y2K nostalgia', 'too much Y2K'). Pixel fonts like Departure Mono are great for one terminal detail and terrible as a brand voice for a compliance product.
  - Radar/spider charts for the M2 severity breakdown. Six axes, no threshold legibility, and the single most over-used chart in AI-scoring product marketing.
  - Screenshotting the proof terminal output as an image. If M1 is the whole differentiator, the stack trace and the base/head run output must be real, selectable, syntax-highlighted text (shiki 4.4.3). A PNG of a terminal is exactly what a product that cannot actually do it would ship.
  - Building the 9-stage scroll choreography on CSS `animation-timeline: scroll()`. It is not Baseline — Firefox has blocked availability since September 2025 and does not ship it in stable. Your signature sequence would simply not animate for Firefox judges.
  - Shipping a trial/unlicensed webfont. Cerebrium is currently serving 'ABC Favorit Trial' in production. In a hackathon you will be looked at closely; use OFL faces (Geist, Inter, IBM Plex Serif/Mono/Sans, JetBrains Mono, Mona Sans, Monaspace, Commit Mono, Instrument Serif, Anybody) and there is no exposure.
  - A generic spinner or a percentage counter preloader. Loaders survived in the winner set only where they do a job (why.zero.university's 'webgl-loader-overlay') or carry the brand (era-residence's theme-aware 'master-preloader'). A spinning ring in front of a page that is already interactive is pure cost.
FACT-CHECK CORRECTIONS:
    [CORRECTED] Could not verify the ITF Free Font License text for Fontshare faces (Satoshi/Switzer); fontshare.com/licenses/itf-ffl returned no license body — verify before shipping Satoshi -> The researcher's caution is half-resolved. The license TEXT is still unreachable — fontshare.com is a client-rendered SPA and /licenses/itf-ffl, /licenses/itf-ffl.txt and the CDN mirror all return the same 691-byte JS shell. But the Fontshare public API does expose the license assignment: GET https://api.fontshare.com/v2/fonts?q=satoshi returns `"license_type": "itf_ffl"`, publisher "Indian Type Foundry", designer Deni Anggara, and `"trials_enabled": true`. Switzer returns the identical `license_type: itf_ffl`. So both faces ARE under ITF FFL and Fontshare self-describes as "a free fonts service from the Indian Type Foundry (ITF)" — but no one has read the actual clause text, and `trials_enabled: true` means trial builds of the same families also circulate. Treat as "license class confirmed, terms unread."
    [CORRECTED] Berkeley Mono / TX-02 pricing: no price appeared in the extracted text; I make no pricing claim; do not assume it is free -> Now verified — it is definitively PAID with no free tier, and the pricing is worse than the researcher's silence implies. usgraphics.com is behind Cloudflare (403 to curl and WebFetch), which is why the fetch came back empty; a real browser session resolves it. FX-102 "Developer" = $75.00, and its spec sheet says "Commercial Use: No". The cheapest COMMERCIAL tier is FX-202 "Indie" (sole proprietor) at $225.00, and web fonts are a separate module: MX-013 Web Fonts +$145.00. So shipping Berkeley Mono on a marketing site costs at least $370 for a sole proprietor, more for FX-400 Startup / FX-600 Medium / FX-700 Enterprise. The 'Avoid Berkeley Mono' recommendation is correct but the stated reason ('I could not verify current pricing') should be replaced with the real one.
    [REFUTED] digitalstrategyforce.com statistics (61% of Q1 2026 SOTD winners immersive 3D vs 23% in Q1 2024; 29 of 47 winners using Three.js; 8.7 vs 6.4 creativity scores; <5% of immersive winners from tech/SaaS; 8% using video backgrounds vs 40%+ in 2022; 97-98% WebGL 2.0) are from a single SEO-style source and could not be corroborated -> The researcher was right to distrust these but too gentle — the numbers are internally self-contradictory and should be dropped entirely, not merely caveated. The source article exists: digitalstrategyforce.com/journal/why-are-immersive-experiences-dominating-the-2026-awwwards/, published 2026-03-12, no named author, no stated methodology. The site is an SEO/AEO services agency (title: "3D Web Design, AEO, GEO, & SEO Services"). Three independent failures: (1) it claims "47 Site of the Day winners in Q1 2026" — SOTD is one per day, so Q1 has ~90 winners, not 47; (2) its own breakdown is 29 Three.js + 8 custom WebGL + 4 Babylon.js + 6 with no WebGL = 47, i.e. 41/47 = 87% WebGL, which flatly contradicts its own headline 61% figure; (3) the WebGL 2.0 number is wrong — caniuse's own data (which the article cites as its source) reports 95.73% global support today, not 97-98%. Separately, my own census of all 31 SOTD winners in the 23 Aug - 22 Sep 2026 window found 13/31 (42%) shipping a <video> tag in server HTML, which is nowhere near the article's "less than 8 percent of 2026 winners" video figure.
    [CORRECTED] View Transitions hit Baseline 'Newly Available' on 2025-10-14 (Chrome 111+, Safari 18+, Firefox 144+) and are used on only ~1.34% of page loads -> The Baseline half is CONFIRMED exactly: web-features `view-transitions` reports baseline "low" with baseline_low_date "2025-10-14" and support {chrome 111, chrome_android 111, edge 111, firefox 144, firefox_android 144, safari 18, safari_ios 18} — every number the researcher gave is right to the version. caniuse independently reports 90.2% global support. The ~1.34% page-loads figure is UNVERIFIABLE — that is a Chrome use-counter statistic and I could not retrieve it from chromestatus.com's API within this session. Drop the 1.34% from any slide; the Baseline date and version floors are solid and are all the recommendation actually needs.
    [UNVERIFIABLE] The 'Awwwards juror' quotes and named sites (By-Kin, Iventions, Mat Voyce, Uncommon Studio, Minh Pham; the 18fps / 9MB / six-second claim) come from hontran.dev, a personal blog claiming juror status. Unverified. -> hontran.dev exists and its header does carry an "Awwwards Jury" credential for author Hon Tran, so the juror claim itself is at least self-asserted on a real site. But I could not find the post containing the quotes: the fetched page shows project listings and testimonials, with no blog articles and no occurrence of 18fps, 9MB, six seconds, By-Kin, Iventions, Mat Voyce, Uncommon Studio or Minh Pham. The quoted juror warning used as the load-bearing evidence in recommendation #6 ('a 3D hero that drops to 18fps on a mid-range Android will not win') should be removed from any judge-facing artifact — it is a single unlocatable secondhand quote from a personal site.
    [REFUTED] Library census over 21 Awwwards SOTD winners dated 23 Aug - 22 Sep 2026: lenis 13/21, motion 9/21, Rive 7/21, GSAP 3/21, Three 3/21, Lottie 2/21; loaders 8/21; custom cursors 2/21; audio 0/21. Direction (Lenis/Motion/Rive over GSAP/Three) is strong. -> I re-ran this census independently over ALL 31 SOTD winners in that exact window (scraped from awwwards.com/websites/sites_of_the_day/, grepping initial server HTML with strict module-specifier patterns). Three problems. (1) DENOMINATOR: there are 30-31 SOTD winners between 23 Aug and 22 Sep 2026, not 21 — the researcher sampled ~68% of the window while describing it as the window. (2) RIVE IS A FALSE POSITIVE. My strict pattern (rive-app | @rive | .riv file | rive.js/wasm) found Rive on exactly 1 of 31 sites (decathlonyestalgia.com, which loads a real `.riv`). A naive substring grep for "rive" matches "are driven by", "jet drive for", "sion drive", and hashed asset names like `ZutRxHRiVeDK34` — on boc.studio, all 4 "rive" hits are exactly these artifacts. The 7/21 figure is almost certainly a substring-grep error. (3) GSAP AND MOTION ARE INVERTED. My counts: lenis 18/31 (58%), GSAP 7/31 (23%), motion 5/31 (16%), three 3/31, lottie 3/31. GSAP is MORE common than Motion in the current winner set, not 3x less. The one direction that survives intact is Lenis's dominance. NOTE: recommendation #2's CONCLUSION (use Motion, not GSAP ScrollTrigger) is still correct — Motion is MIT, you are already in React, and GSAP carries a bespoke non-SPDX license — but it must be re-argued on those grounds, because the empirical premise ('winner stack is Lenis+Motion+Rive 13/9/7 with GSAP at 3') does not replicate.
    [CORRECTED] Colour conversions from CSS lab() to hex: CodeRabbit lab(4.9349 1.26021 -1.99696) -> #111014; Graphite lab(2.75381 0 0) -> #0A0A0A; Graphite text lab(98.26 0 0) -> #FAFAFA; Warp card lab(2.48968 -.45754 -.465265) -> #07090A. Computed under D65; may be off by ~1 per channel. -> Three of four exact, two off by exactly 1 in the red channel — and the researcher named the cause correctly. Recomputing per CSS Color 4 (lab() uses a D50 white point, then Bradford-adapted to D65 sRGB): Graphite bg #0A0A0A CONFIRMED; Graphite text #FAFAFA CONFIRMED; CodeRabbit is #121014, not #111014; Warp card is #08090A, not #07090A. The D65 assumption in the researcher's method is the bug — CSS lab() is specified against D50. Use #121014 and #08090A if you are matching these exactly.
    [CORRECTED] Paper Shaders: v0.0.81, published 2026-09-17, Apache-2.0, 3,471 stars, zero runtime dependencies, canvas (not Three.js), with the listed 30 components -> Almost entirely confirmed, with one precision fix. Version 0.0.81 CONFIRMED, published 2026-09-17T09:52:46Z CONFIRMED to the day, Apache-2.0 CONFIRMED on both npm and the repo. Stars: github.com/paper-design/shaders reports aria-label="3471 users starred this repository" — CONFIRMED to the exact number. Repo tagline is literally "Zero-dependency canvas shaders", confirming canvas not Three.js. All 30 component names in the researcher's list appear on shaders.paper.design with no omissions and no extras. THE FIX: @paper-design/shaders-react is not literally zero-dependency — it depends on @paper-design/shaders@0.0.81 (its own first-party core), which in turn has `dependencies: {}`. So it is zero THIRD-PARTY dependencies across two packages. Peer deps are react ^18||^19 and @types/react ^18||^19, which is fine for Next 16.
    [CORRECTED] Linear specifics: body #08090A, --bg-base #0f1011, --bg-shade #191d20, --color-bg-panel #161718, text #F7F8F8; card edge inset 0 0 0 1px #ffffff08 + inset 0 1px #ffffff0a; pointer-tracked --edge-highlight-x/y; grain = static PNG tile on .grainOverlay; easing cubic-bezier(.32,.72,0,1) x7; syntax tokens #8fa6ff/#83dcdc; 0 canvas 0 video above fold; Inter at 510; h1 64/64/-1.408px; header backdrop-filter saturate(1.8) blur(20px) -> The load-bearing parts of recommendation #7 all CONFIRM. Verified present in Linear's production CSS: #08090A, #0f1011, #191d20, #161718, #F7F8F8; the exact strings `inset 0 0 0 1px #ffffff08` and `inset 0 1px #ffffff0a`; `--edge-highlight-x` (15 occurrences); syntax tokens #8fa6ff and #83dcdc. The grain claim is confirmed precisely — the rule is `.grainOverlay{z-index:2;background-image:url(https://static.linear.app/web/_next/static/media/grain-default.mxaw2efG.png)}`, a static PNG, the ONLY .png in Linear's CSS. 0 <canvas> and 0 <video> in server HTML CONFIRMED. Easing count is 5, not 7, in the 25 stylesheets I could pull (the researcher's caveat about unfetched stylesheets applies — Linear's CSS is code-split and I captured only ~120KB). THREE CLAIMS I COULD NOT FIND: `font-weight: 510` does not appear anywhere in the CSS I retrieved (only font-weight:500, 6x); `saturate(1.8) blur(20px)` does not appear (the header uses `backdrop-filter: blur(var(--header-blur))`); and `letter-spacing: -1.408px` does not appear. Treat the 510 weight, the saturate value and the h1 metrics as UNVERIFIED computed-style readings. Also note Linear's live h1 is now "The product development system for teams and agents" — the site has been updated since any earlier snapshot.
    [CORRECTED] Vercel: --ds-background-100 hsla(0,0%,4%,1)=#0A0A0A, --ds-background-200 #000, --ds-gray-100 #1A1A1A, --ds-gray-1000 #EDEDED, --ds-blue-900 oklch(71.7% 0.1648 250.794), --ds-shadow-border '0 0 0 1px #ffffff25' -> Values right, one token NAME wrong. Confirmed in production CSS: `--ds-background-100-value: 0, 0%, 4%` (= #0A0A0A) and `--ds-background-200-value: 0, 0%, 0%`; `--ds-gray-1000:#ededed`; `--ds-gray-100:#1A1A1A`; `--ds-blue-900:oklch(71.7% .1648 250.794)` exactly. THE FIX: the token is `--ds-shadow-border-base: 0 0 0 1px #ffffff25`, not `--ds-shadow-border`. `--ds-shadow-border` exists but is a composite that references the base. There is also a sibling worth knowing for recommendation #7: `--ds-shadow-border-inset: inset 0 0 0 1px #ffffff1a` (dark) — 0x1a, not 0x08, so Vercel's inset hairline is brighter than Linear's. Minor ambiguity: a hex-fallback declaration `--ds-background-100:#000` also exists alongside the hsla-based one, so #0A0A0A is defensible but not the only value Vercel ships. Vercel's live h1 is now "Agentic Infrastructure".
    [CORRECTED] Modal: --semantic-background-{success,warning,caution,danger,running} token naming; h1 'Goga' in rgb(221,255,220) = #DDFFDC; 8 videos, 2 canvases; Degular / ABC Diatype / DM Mono -> Token system CONFIRMED and it is the best part of the recommendation: --semantic-background-running (16 occurrences), -success (19), -caution (16) all present, so 'running' really is a first-class semantic colour. #DDFFDC confirmed 39 times in CSS. Goga, Degular, ABC Diatype and DM Mono all present. 8 <video> CONFIRMED exactly. ONE CORRECTION: I count 1 <canvas> in server HTML, not 2 — the second is presumably created at runtime. Immaterial to the recommendation.
    [CORRECTED] boc.studio: body #181D21; ONE canvas at 2880x346; PP Mori type; stack Lenis + Motion + Rive (no GSAP, no Three); one video; no preloader -> Mostly confirmed, but the Rive part is a false positive. Confirmed: #181D21 (4x in CSS), `font-family:ppMori` (PP Mori confirmed), `lenis` present, `"motion"` present as a module specifier, 1 <video>, 0 GSAP, 0 Three, 0 <canvas> in server HTML (runtime-created). REFUTED: boc.studio does NOT load Rive. The four "rive" substring hits are the hashed asset name `ZutRxHRiVeDK34` (3x) and the phrase "are driven by" (1x). Strict matching for rive-app / @rive / .riv / rive.js finds nothing. Awwwards SOTD 19 Sep 2026 confirmed.
    [CORRECTED] Cursor: body rgb(20,18,11) = #14120B; bespoke CursorGothic; --font-serif falling back to Iowan Old Style / Palatino; palette #EDECEC, #CAC6BE, #47433C, #3C3935, #26251E, #C08532, #C9A227, #14120B; serves logo-dark-theme.mp4; zero canvases -> Two specifics are wrong. CONFIRMED: #14120B, CursorGothic (15x CSS / 6x HTML), Iowan Old Style, #EDECEC (24x), #CAC6BE (5x), #47433C (5x), #3C3935 (4x), #26251E (54x), #C08532 (5x), 0 <canvas>. REFUTED: #C9A227 appears ZERO times in either the HTML or the concatenated CSS — that hex is not in the current palette. REFUTED: `logo-dark-theme.mp4` does not exist on the page; the only video asset Cursor serves is `/marketing-static/manifest.webm`, and there are 0 <video> tags in server HTML. Drop both from any palette or asset reference. Live h1 is "Cursor is your coding agent for building ambitious software."
    [UNVERIFIABLE] Dimension: body #0C0C0C; h1 Geist 500 48px/48px with letter-spacing normal; 8 videos, 0 canvases, 17 mix-blend elements, 18 backdrop-filter rules; easings cubic-bezier(.08,.82,.17,1) and cubic-bezier(.33,1,.68,1) -> Cannot verify any of it from the server. www.dimension.dev 301s to dimension.dev, which returns an 8,324-byte Next.js shell with no h1, no <video>, no <canvas> and no linked stylesheets — everything is client-rendered. So all of the researcher's Dimension numbers came from a rendered browser session I cannot reproduce here. More importantly the product has repositioned: the current title and og:title are "Dimension — Your AI work assistant" with description "Connected to your apps. Understands your work. Gets things done on your behalf." It is no longer the design/dev-tool site the survey frames it as, so its citation as a 'dark product site with 8 videos and 0 canvases' in recommendation #6 rests on an unverifiable and possibly stale snapshot.
    [CORRECTED] 8 of 21 current Awwwards winners ship a loader (38%), including why.zero.university's literally-named 'webgl-loader-overlay' and era-residence's theme-aware 'master-preloader theme_on-dark' -> Both named examples are real; the ratio is understated. why.zero.university does ship an element literally named `webgl-loader-overlay` (plus `LoaderTextOverlay` and `loaderGradient`) — CONFIRMED verbatim, and it was SOTD on 07 Sep 2026. era-residence.com does ship a master preloader, but as DATA ATTRIBUTES `data-master-preloader` and `data-preloader`, not as the class string 'master-preloader theme_on-dark'; I also found `preloader_bg_arch_is-`, `preloader_title-l` and `preloader_title-r`. It was SOTD 31 Aug 2026, inside the window. On prevalence: across all 31 SOTD winners in the window I find loader/preloader markers on 16/31 (52%), not 8/21 (38%) — my pattern is looser so treat 38-52% as the range, but the direction (loaders are alive and roughly half the winner set ships one) is if anything stronger than claimed. Recommendation #8 is safe.
VERIFIER ADDITIONS:
    + BIGGEST FINDING — the Rive number is a grep artifact and it changes a recommendation's evidence. Recommendation #2 says 'empirically the current winner stack is Lenis+Motion+Rive (13/9/7 of 21) with GSAP at 3/21'. I re-ran the census over all 31 SOTD winners in the stated window with strict module-specifier patterns and got lenis 18, GSAP 7, motion 5, three 3, lottie 3, rive 1. Rive appears on exactly one site (decathlonyestalgia.com, a real .riv file). A naive substring grep for 'rive' matches 'are driven by', 'jet drive for', and hashed bundle names like ZutRxHRiVeDK34 — on boc.studio, ALL FOUR 'rive' hits are exactly those. GSAP also outranks Motion, inverting the claimed 9-vs-3. The RECOMMENDATION still holds (Motion is MIT, you are in React, GSAP's license is bespoke and non-SPDX) but if a judge greps the same sites, the stated evidence collapses. Re-argue #2 on license and React-fit, not on census ratios.
    + The census denominator is wrong in a way that is easy for a judge to check. The researcher says '21 Awwwards SOTD winners dated 23 Aug - 22 Sep 2026'. There are 30-31 SOTD winners in that window (SOTD is one per day). I enumerated all of them off awwwards.com/websites/sites_of_the_day/. Presenting 13/21 as if it covered the window invites exactly the 'show me your n' challenge that recommendation #15 says PRGuard's whole thesis is about. Either widen to 31 or state the sample explicitly as '21 of 31 winners sampled'.
    + Berkeley Mono's EULA has a clause that is directly disqualifying for this product, beyond price. usgraphics.com states: 'Commercial licenses are not compatible with open-source apps. Commercial use restricted to UI elements only. If you're building an IDE, Terminal app, Text Editor, etc., we generally do not allow it.' PRGuard's signature moment is rendering real terminal output as selectable text. Even if you paid the $225 Indie + $145 Web Fonts, that use is arguably outside the license. The 'avoid Berkeley Mono' call is right for a stronger reason than the researcher gave.
    + Recommendation #3's core differentiation premise is partly false. It says a cold blue-black with instrument-cyan is 'measurably unoccupied in this competitive set' — but the survey's own data contradicts this: Cerebrium is #101421 (blue-black), Evervault is #010314 (deep blue-black), Railway is #13111C (violet-black), boc.studio is #181D21 (cool slate), and Raycast's accent --color-blue is #57c1ff, a cyan-leaning blue. Non-neutral cold darks are common, not open ground. Worse, the recommendation proposes lifting #7AEBFF + #7AEBFF33 + #FFCB47 + #FF6666 as 'USAvionix's exact pair' — that is the complete accent palette of a site that won Awwwards SOTD on 09 Sep 2026, thirteen days before this survey, built by basement.studio. Copying a current winner's exact accent triple is the single most checkable plagiarism risk in the whole plan. Shift the hue (e.g. toward #6FD8F0 or a colder #5EC8E8) and pick your own warning/blocking values.
    + Three sites in the reference list have repositioned since whatever snapshot informed the survey, which matters because the doc frames them as category examples: Warp's h1 is now 'Open infrastructure for cloud software factories' (it is no longer pitched as a terminal); Linear's h1 is now 'The product development system for teams and agents'; Vercel's h1 is now 'Agentic Infrastructure'; and Dimension is now 'Your AI work assistant'. Dimension in particular serves an 8KB client-rendered shell, so its 8-videos/0-canvases datapoint — one of the three pillars of recommendation #6 — cannot be verified at all. Recommendation #6 still stands on Resend alone, which I verified hard (4 mp4s, 0 canvases, confirmed filenames), plus Cursor (0 canvases, 1 webm) and Linear (0 of either).
    + The researcher's advice to 're-check caniuse' for scroll-driven animations would actively mislead. caniuse's css-scroll-timeline entry is the OBSOLETE @scroll-timeline at-rule: status 'unoff', zero support in every browser including Chrome, 0% usage. The modern animation-timeline/ScrollTimeline feature lives in web-features as 'scroll-driven-animations' (baseline false; chrome/edge 115, safari 26, Firefox absent). The researcher already used the right source and reached the right conclusion — the caveat telling the reader to go back to caniuse should be deleted.
    + Two of the four lab()->hex conversions are off by one in the red channel, and the reason is a spec error worth fixing in the researcher's method: CSS Color 4 defines lab() against a D50 white point, not D65. Recomputing under D50 with Bradford adaptation: CodeRabbit is #121014 (not #111014) and Warp's card is #08090A (not #07090A). Graphite's #0A0A0A and #FAFAFA are exact either way.
    + The digitalstrategyforce.com statistics are not merely uncorroborated — they are internally impossible, and should be struck rather than caveated. The article claims '47 Site of the Day winners in Q1 2026' when Q1 has ~90 days and therefore ~90 SOTDs. Its own component figures (29 Three.js + 8 custom WebGL + 4 Babylon.js + 6 with no WebGL = 47) imply 41/47 = 87% WebGL, directly contradicting its own 61% headline. Its WebGL 2.0 figure of ~97% is wrong against the source it cites (caniuse reports 95.73%). And its '<8% use video backgrounds' is contradicted by my own count of 13/31 SOTD winners shipping a <video> tag. If any of these reach a judge-facing slide, one search kills the credibility of the whole deck — which is the precise failure mode recommendation #15's tradeoff warns about.
    + Two small factual repairs for anything you copy verbatim: the Vercel token is --ds-shadow-border-base (the composite --ds-shadow-border references it), and Vercel's inset hairline is --ds-shadow-border-inset: inset 0 0 0 1px #ffffff1a — noticeably brighter than Linear's #ffffff08, so if you blend the two systems pick one alpha and stay on it. And on Cursor, #C9A227 and logo-dark-theme.mp4 do not exist on the current site; the only Cursor video asset is /marketing-static/manifest.webm.
    + Paper Shaders is in better shape than the 'medium confidence' rating suggests, with one caveat the researcher missed. Version, publish date (2026-09-17), Apache-2.0, the 3,471 star count and all 30 component names check out to the digit. The repo tagline is literally 'Zero-dependency canvas shaders'. But @paper-design/shaders-react does carry one dependency — @paper-design/shaders@0.0.81, its own core, which itself has zero deps. So it is zero THIRD-PARTY deps across two packages, not literally zero dependencies. If you pin, pin both. Peer deps react ^18||^19 are compatible with Next 16.3.6.
    + Recommendation #10's easing frequency claims are the most precisely verified numbers in the survey and every single one replicated exactly: Linear cubic-bezier(.32,.72,0,1) [5 in my 25-stylesheet pull vs their 7, the only gap], Raycast (.23,1,.32,1) x12 / (.4,0,.22,.96) x10 / (.16,1,.3,1) x8, Warp (.16,1,.3,1) x8, CodeRabbit (.23,1,.32,1) x11 / (.22,1,.36,1) x8, basement (.4,0,.2,1) x14, e2b (.32,.72,0,1) x6 / (.23,1,.32,1) x3, Oxide (.87,0,.13,1) x4, Warp's 619 color-mix() and Resend's 898 color-mix(). Whoever did this measurement did it properly; the failures are concentrated in the library census and the third-party statistics, not in the CSS work.

### AREA: Dribbble / Behance / visual trend research — dark AI SaaS + dashboards (dribbble-visual)
FINDINGS:
  - Dribbble's OWN 2026 editorial thesis is 'clarity through restraint', not glow. The Dribbble Select year-end roundup promotes BioForge (Phenomenon Studio), Shift Robotics (IDOL), Freeport Markets (Eloqwnt), Cerebra Cybersecurity (Wavespace) and AI Simulation Platform (Excited) — and describes every one of them with the same three words: 'restrained color palette, bold typography, structured layouts'. Freeport Markets is explicitly praised for looking like institutional finance rather than crypto. The orb-and-gradient shot is no longer what the platform itself platforms.
  - The Muzli '50 best dashboards for 2026' survey is one formula repeated ~50 times: deep charcoal/near-black base + exactly ONE saturated neon accent + frosted-glass widgets, often over a cinematic photograph. Repeat sources: Orbix Studio (Vaulto — 'deep charcoal background' + Sankey diagram; OpsPulse — 'frosted-glass aesthetic, vibrant blurred background'; NobleFinance — 'refined dark palette and energetic green highlights'), Airzon Agency (InsightStream — 'high-contrast neon-on-dark, vibrant pink and green accents'), Juice Lab (frosted glass over aerial photography). The glass-over-photo move is the single clearest Dribbble-only tell.
  - The real reference the shots are imitating is Linear's shipped token set, and it is fetchable. Dark ladder: --color-bg-level-0 #08090a, level-1 #0f1011, level-2 #141516, level-3 #191a1b, --color-bg-marketing #010102, --color-bg-panel #0f1011. Borders: --color-border-translucent #ffffff0d, --color-border-translucent-strong #ffffff14, opaque #23252a / #34343a / #3e3e44. Text: #f7f8f8 / #d0d6e0 / #8a8f98 / #62666d. Accent #7170ff, hover #828fff, brand #5e6ad2. Radii: --radius-4/12/16/24/32 — there is NO 8px step.
  - Linear's 'expensive' hairline is two facts, not one: --border-hairline is 1px but drops to .5px at >=2x device-pixel-ratio, and the section seam is `border-bottom: 1px solid var(--color-border-translucent); box-shadow: 0 1px #0006` — a light 1px on top of a black 1px. That pair is what makes a dark page read as machined rather than flat.
  - Linear ships four named surface effects as separate CSS modules (Grain.css, EdgeShine.css) with exact numbers worth copying: grain = `background-size:256px 256px; mix-blend-mode:overlay; opacity:.9` plus a `#ffffff0f` lift layer that is explicitly killed in Safari via `@supports((-webkit-hyphens:none))`; grainSubtle = opacity .6 with no lift layer. edgeHighlight masks a `#ffffff0f` inherited border with `radial-gradient(ellipse 17.18% 28.57% at 0 0)` — a corner specular. edgeGlow tints `#ffffff08` through a 7-stop color-mix falloff (94/78/58/35/16/3%). `.shine` is a 200px mouse-follow beam driven by --mask-x/--mask-y.
  - The 1px gradient border that reads as premium is the mask-composite trick, verbatim from Linear: `padding: var(--gradientBorder-size,1px); background: var(--gradientBorder-gradient); mask-image: linear-gradient(#000,#000), linear-gradient(#000,#000); mask-clip: content-box, border-box; mask-composite: exclude`. Resend ships the animated variant — `conic-gradient(from var(--angle) at 50% 50%, ...)` rotated via a registered --angle custom property.
  - Linear ships a `.slashedZero` utility — `font-variant-numeric: lining-nums tabular-nums slashed-zero` — and sets `--font-settings: "cv01","ss03"` on Inter Variable (cv01 = 'Alternate one', the disambiguated 1; ss03 = round quotes & commas), with `--font-variations: "opsz" auto`. Weights are 300 / 400 / 510 / 590 / 680, not 400/500/600/700. Their mono is 'Berkeley Mono'. Type scale: 11 / 12 / 13 / 15 / 18px, titles 20 / 24 / 36px.
  - shadcn/ui's default `.dark` block — which every hackathon entry will ship untouched — is `--background: oklch(0.145 0 0)`, `--card: oklch(0.205 0 0)`, `--border: oklch(1 0 0 / 10%)`, `--destructive: oklch(0.704 0.191 22.216)` and a chart ramp of oklch(0.488 0.243 264.376) / (0.696 0.17 162.48) / (0.769 0.188 70.08) / (0.627 0.265 303.9) / (0.645 0.246 16.439). Cross-checked against tailwindcss@4.3.3's theme.css, chart-2 IS emerald-500, chart-3 IS amber-500 and chart-4 IS purple-500, exactly. Shipping that ramp is a visible 'generated in a weekend' signature.
  - The purple tell has a traceable origin and a current value: it is Tailwind's indigo-500, today `oklch(58.5% 0.233 277.117)` in tailwindcss@4.3.3, with violet-500 `oklch(60.6% 0.25 292.717)` and purple-500 `oklch(62.7% 0.265 303.9)`. Three independent 2026 sources (impeccable.style/slop, 925studios, indiehackers 'AI Purple Problem') name the indigo-to-purple gradient as the loudest AI tell of the year.
  - CodeRabbit's actual shipped palette, pulled from their homepage markup: purple-tinted neutrals #332f39 / #34303a / #302d36 / #302c35 / #242129 / #403946, text #f4f1f5 / #aaa3ae / #85808c / #d6d0d7, brand orange #f04006, mint #65e6a6 with tints #d6f9df and #203929, plus #f87171 / #fb923c / #facc15 / #93c5fd / #a78bfa (raw Tailwind). Their dark is a WARM purple-grey with an orange brand. Any PRGuard palette that is cool-neutral with a non-orange accent will read as a different, more clinical product at a glance.
  - The direct competitors are on the same three fonts. cubic.dev is a Framer site running Geist (300/500/regular) + Fragment Mono + Inter, with card borders at rgb(40,40,44) = #28282c — byte-identical to Linear's --color-bg-quaternary. graphite.dev runs Inter + JetBrains Mono + DM Mono + 'Matter' with --base-border-radius at 2 / 6 / 8 / 12px. CodeRabbit declares `--font-cui-mono: Hack`. Typography is genuinely open ground in this category.
  - Infra and security brands are NOT a safe harbour from purple: depot.dev leads with #00aaff + #a033ff / #a641ff, warp.dev uses lavender #c7aeff on #0b0b0b / #1f1f1f, chainguard.dev runs magenta #eb02e0 and violet #6226fb over a blue-black #0d161c / #192228 with orange #f85722. The 'serious' corner of devtools is as purple as the AI corner.
  - The animation budget is now free at full fidelity: gsap is at 3.15.0 under a 'Standard no charge license' with all ex-Club plugins (SplitText, MorphSVG, DrawSVG, ScrollTrigger) included after Webflow's acquisition. Current peers verified on npm 2026-09-22: motion 13.4.1 (MIT), three 0.186.0 (MIT), @react-three/fiber 9.7.0 (MIT), lenis 1.3.26 (MIT), tailwindcss 4.3.3, next 16.3.6, react 19.3.0, lucide-react 1.47.0 (ISC), @phosphor-icons/react 2.1.10 (MIT), recharts 3.10.1 (MIT), shiki 4.4.3 (MIT), geist 1.7.2 (SIL OFL), uplot 1.6.32 (MIT).
  - Runtime cost ranking for the 'cinematic hero': Rive's web runtime is ~200KB gzipped (one-time, shared); Unicorn Studio's is ~29KB gzipped; a Three.js/R3F scene is heaviest and browsers cap WebGL contexts at ~16 per page. Codrops' own 2026 guidance is that if a linear gradient solves it, WebGL is not worth the runtime cost. For a 9-stage pipeline, hand-authored inline SVG (typically <10KB) using stroke-dasharray/stroke-dashoffset for flowing conduits plus animateMotion for travelling packets beats all three — and, decisively for THIS product, it lives in git and gets reviewed in the same PR as the code.
  - Bento is now table stakes, not differentiation: SaaSFrame's 2026 guide puts adoption at ~67% of top-tier SaaS landing pages, default for new B2B homepage launches since ~May 2026, with the 2026 evolution being 'active bento' (tile opens, plays video, or reveals a second layer on hover) rather than a colour change. Using a full-page bento in September 2026 signals 'we used the template'.
  - Glassmorphism has been demoted from headline trend to supporting element for 2026, and its Dribbble form (frosted panels over a cinematic photograph) is precisely the part that fails in product: backdrop-filter over live scrolling content costs GPU on every frame and destroys text contrast the moment real data scrolls beneath it.
  - Dark-mode physiology argues against the shot aesthetic: #FFFFFF on #000000 is 21:1 — the maximum possible and more than the eye's optics need — and maximises halation; roughly a third of US adults have astigmatism. Material's dark baseline is #121212 with elevation overlays scaling from 0% white at 0dp to 16% at 24dp. Linear's shipped answer is the practical one: never #000 (they use #08090a), never #fff (they use #f7f8f8).
  - Two cheap, category-differentiating typographic facts for a proof product: only ~16% of web fonts ship both tabular and proportional numeral variants (2024 Web Almanac via HTTP Archive), and `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums slashed-zero` on a proportional face gives aligned numeric columns with better readability than full monospace. Every severity score (0.00-1.00), token count, cost figure and fingerprint prefix in PRGuard should be tabular + slashed-zero. Audit-trail interfaces have four established visual grammars — list (scan volume), detail (deep evidence), timeline (progression), aggregated (patterns/KPIs) — and PRGuard's hash chain needs timeline + detail, not a table.

Prescriptions: 65-75 character line length, ~1.5 line-height, 16px body floor, WCAG AA 4.5:1 / 3:1, one solid colour with size/weight for emphasis rather than gradient text, spacing and dividers instead of nested containers, transforms for motion.

Ship smarter.' Counterexamples named: Linear ('custom-feeling type and colour system'), Stripe ('deliberate typographic and colour decisions'), Duolingo ('loud, character-driven visual language'). Fix: asymmetry and a different rhythm; a headline only your product could say.
  - [strong] Material Design 2 — dark theme surfaces and elevation https://m2.material.io/design/color/dark-theme.html :: STEAL: The elevation-as-lightness model (raise a surface by adding white, never by adding a drop shadow) and the desaturation rule for accents on dark. :: SPECIFICS: Baseline dark surface #121212; elevation overlays scale from 0% white at 0dp to 16% white at 24dp; desaturate primary colours on dark because saturated colours 'visually vibrate' against dark surfaces. Corroborating accessibility research: #FFF on #000 is 21:1 (more than the eye's optics need) and maximises halation; ~33% of US adults have astigmatism; recommended off-white text in the #CCCCCC-#D4D4D4 region rather than #FFFFFF.
  - [must-steal] Aviation display colour semantics (FAR 25.1322 + Applied Avionics tech guide) https://www.appliedavionics.com/techguides/Content/TG-LPBS-21/1.3.1_Aviation%20Colors.htm :: STEAL: A century-old, legally codified, non-AI colour language for exactly PRGuard's problem: states of certainty and authority. This is the differentiated alternative to purple-means-smart. :: SPECIFICS: FAR 25.1322: red = warning, amber/yellow = caution, advisory = any colour except red or green. Industry practice on EFIS: green = pilot-selected data and mode annunciations, magenta = target/commanded information, cyan = background / non-selectable data (Bombardier also uses cyan for loss of redundancy). Chromaticity is specified on the CIE diagram (e.g. blue x .100-.180 / y .145-.250; red x .655-.710 / y .285-.352), with contrast per MIL-PRF-22885 for sunlight-readable Type S displays.
  - [strong] Resend — animated conic gradient border https://resend.com :: STEAL: The rotating-conic border as a 'live/running' affordance — perfect for a review run that is currently executing, and far cheaper than a WebGL glow. :: SPECIFICS: `--border-color: conic-gradient(from var(--angle) at 50% 50%, ...)` over a `--bg-color: linear-gradient(#000,#000)` base, animated by registering --angle with @property and rotating 0turn -> 1turn. Pair with the mask-composite 1px gradient-border technique so the conic only paints the border.
  - [strong] GSAP 3.15.0 — now free including all former Club plugins https://webflow.com/blog/gsap-becomes-free :: STEAL: ScrollTrigger for the 9-stage pipeline scroll choreography and SplitText for the headline reveal — at zero cost and zero licence risk for a commercial hackathon entry. :: SPECIFICS: npm `gsap` is at 3.15.0, licence field reads "Standard 'no charge' license: https://gsap.com/standard-license". Webflow acquired GreenSock Oct 2024 and made the full toolset free in April 2025, including SplitText, MorphSVG, DrawSVG and ScrollTrigger. SplitText was rewritten in 3.13 with a ~50% file-size reduction.
  - [strong] Runtime-cost comparison for 'cinematic' heroes (Rive / Unicorn Studio / Three.js) https://tympanus.net/codrops/2026/03/04/webgl-for-designers-creating-interactive-shader-driven-graphics-directly-in-the-browser/ :: STEAL: The decision rule: SVG first, Rive if it must be 2D-interactive, WebGL only for the one hero moment — and never more than one WebGL context. :: SPECIFICS: Unicorn Studio runtime ~29KB gzipped with a built-in performance estimator, layer downsampling, DPI/FPS clamping, auto-pause off-screen, and layer flattening to cut draw calls. Rive web runtime (WASM + JS) ~200KB gzipped, one-time and shared across all animations on the page. Three.js is heaviest; browsers cap WebGL contexts at ~16 per page. Codrops' rule: if a simple linear gradient solves the problem, do not reach for WebGL. Hand-authored SVG is typically under 10KB, styleable in the site's own tokens, diffable in git.
RECOMMENDATIONS:
  - (high) Ship an 'instrument black + avionics semantics' palette and delete every purple from the project. Base ladder (cool neutral with a nearly-imperceptible green cast that kills any purple read): --surface-void oklch(0.145 0.004 165) ~#080B0A, --surface-base oklch(0.178 0.005 165) ~#0D110F, --surface-1 oklch(0.212 0.006 165) ~#131816, --surface-2 oklch(0.245 0.006 165) ~#191F1D, --surface-3 oklch(0.278 0.006 165). Borders: hairline oklch(1 0 0 / 0.07), strong oklch(1 0 0 / 0.13), interactive oklch(1 0 0 / 0.22). Text: oklch(0.97 0.004 165) / 0.80 / 0.635 / 0.50. Then bind the FIVE proof states to the aviation language rather than to brand whim: PROVEN = oklch(0.80 0.16 148) (a cold verified green, deliberately off emerald-500's hue 162 so it does not read as shadcn); DISPROVEN / self-refuted = oklch(0.81 0.15 88) amber, because amber is codified as caution not failure and self-refutation is the product's proudest move, not its shame; INCONCLUSIVE / NOT_ATTEMPTED = oklch(0.74 0.09 218) cyan, because cyan is codified as background / non-selectable data; BLOCKING = oklch(0.64 0.21 26) red, used at most once per screen and ONLY on a proven finding; HUMAN OVERRIDE / break-glass = oklch(0.74 0.19 332) magenta, because magenta is codified as commanded/target — i.e. a human told it to. Document this mapping on the site. It is a story no competitor can tell. | WHY: Three independent 2026 sources name the indigo-to-purple gradient as the loudest AI tell of the year and trace it to Tailwind indigo-500. CodeRabbit is warm-purple-neutral + orange; cubic, Depot, Warp and Chainguard are all purple-adjacent. A cool near-neutral base with an amber/cyan/green/magenta state language is simultaneously (a) unoccupied in this category, (b) legible as 'instrument' rather than 'magic', and (c) backed by a real, citable, 50-year-old safety standard — which is itself on-message for a product whose whole pitch is provenance. | TRADEOFF: Green-on-black risks a 'hacker terminal' or Matrix read if the chroma goes above ~0.02 on surfaces; keep surface chroma at 0.004-0.006 so the cast is felt, not seen. Amber-for-disproven is counterintuitive on first read and needs a legend in the UI. The five-state palette costs you one accent slot for pure brand expression — the brand has to live in typography and form instead.
  - (high) Make the hero a working artefact, not a decoration. No orb, no blob, no mesh-gradient aurora. The above-the-fold moment is a two-pane terminal: LEFT = base SHA, test PASSES, green; RIGHT = head SHA, same test, FAILS, with a real stack trace — typed out on load with real ANSI colour, then a hash-chained audit line stamping underneath it. Render it with shiki 4.4.3 for genuine tokenization (not a hand-faked <span> soup), keep the whole thing under 40KB, and let the copy be the headline: 'Other reviewers say it might. We ran it.' The 3D/cinematic budget goes into ONE thing only — the 9-stage pipeline diagram — and nothing else. | WHY: The brief's own signature demo moment is 'Tool A says this MIGHT null-deref. We say it DOES.' The fastest way to look more expensive than CodeRabbit is not more glow, it is showing a thing that is obviously real while they show an illustration. Dribbble's own 2026 editorial (BioForge, Freeport Markets) rewards exactly this 'credibility over magic' register, and impeccable.style names the glowing orb and the radial halo as slop by name. | TRADEOFF: A terminal hero is less immediately 'pretty' in a thumbnail than a rendered 3D scene, so it must be typographically immaculate to carry the frame — and it needs real, non-embarrassing sample output, which means you must actually run the demo repo and capture it. It also puts a lot of weight on copy quality.
  - (high) Build the 9-stage pipeline as hand-authored inline SVG in the site's own CSS tokens, driven by GSAP 3.15.0 ScrollTrigger — not Spline, not Three.js, not Rive. Technique: each stage is a node with a 1px hairline ring; conduits between stages are paths animated with stroke-dasharray/stroke-dashoffset; work in flight is a small packet travelling the path via animateMotion; stage state is colour only (idle = border hairline, running = conic-rotating border, complete = proven green, degraded = amber). The scroll choreography pins the diagram and walks S1->S9 with the stage label, heartbeat age and stage_index/stage_total rendered in mono beside it. The four specialists in S3 must visibly run in PARALLEL — four simultaneous packets — because that is a real architectural claim. | WHY: The diagram IS the product story, and it must be the same diagram in the marketing site and the dashboard's live run view. SVG at <10KB lives in git, re-themes from the same tokens as the app, animates at native resolution, and gets reviewed in the PR — which is a quietly perfect meta-argument for a code-review company. Rive costs ~200KB of runtime and Unicorn Studio ~29KB before you draw anything; a Three.js scene costs a WebGL context and a mobile frame budget for a diagram that is fundamentally 2D. | TRADEOFF: Hand-authored SVG is more engineering hours up front than dropping in a Spline scene, and you lose easy depth/lighting. If you want any genuine 3D, spend it on one small object (an embossed seal / a stacked evidence ledger) rendered once as a static asset rather than a live scene.
  - (high) Refuse the Inter default at least at display size. Use a two-voice system: (1) a mono as the BRAND voice, not just as a code font — Geist Mono (SIL OFL, npm geist@1.7.2) or JetBrains Mono (OFL) set at 11/12/13px with letter-spacing ~0.02em and uppercase for every label, stage name, fingerprint prefix, SHA, severity input name and audit event type; (2) a grotesk for prose. If you keep Inter for prose, you must at minimum ship Linear's settings — font-feature-settings: 'cv01','ss03' (disambiguated 1, round quotes) plus font-variant-numeric: lining-nums tabular-nums slashed-zero everywhere a number appears — and use its variable weights at 510/590/680 rather than 500/600/700. Berkeley Mono is the highest-end option if the budget exists (verify current pricing yourself; their site is Cloudflare-gated). | WHY: 925studios names 'Inter everywhere' as the number-one AI tell and impeccable.style repeats it. Meanwhile CodeRabbit ships Hack, cubic ships Geist + Fragment Mono, Graphite ships Inter + JetBrains + DM Mono — typography is genuinely unclaimed ground in this category. And for a product whose atoms are hashes, SHAs, weighted scores and line numbers, a mono-led identity is not decoration, it is the literal subject matter. Only ~16% of web fonts support tabular figures, so declaring tabular-nums + slashed-zero is both cheap and visibly rigorous. | TRADEOFF: Mono at UI scale reads slower for prose and eats horizontal space — keep it to labels, identifiers and numerics, never to paragraphs. Uppercase mono labels tank readability if they exceed about three words. Geist is strongly associated with Vercel, which is a mild borrowed-identity risk; JetBrains Mono is more neutral but more common.
  - (high) Do NOT visualise M2 computed severity as a radar chart, even though the six inputs beg for one. Use a single stacked horizontal contribution bar: six segments whose widths are weight x input, summing to the final score, with the band threshold ticks (.15 / .35 / .55 / .75) printed on the axis as hairline marks and the achieved band labelled. Under it, the human-readable sentence, and under that a collapsible table of input | raw 0..1 | weight | contribution, all tabular-nums. Hover a segment, it highlights the corresponding clause in the sentence. | WHY: The severity score is a weighted SUM, and a stacked bar is the only mark that is faithful to addition — a radar encodes by area and will make a .74 look like a .55 depending on which axes are hot, which directly undermines the product's central claim of determinism. It is also far more legible at the 320px width it will occupy in a finding card. The threshold ticks are the move that makes the whole mechanic self-explaining in one glance: you can see it clear .75 and go critical. | TRADEOFF: A radar chart is more visually arresting in a hackathon screenshot and is what a Dribbble shot would do. You give up that instant 'ooh' for something that actually reads. Mitigate by making the bar animate its segments in on reveal, in stage order, with the number counting up.
  - (medium) Design the M4 audit chain as a vertical ledger, using the timeline + detail grammar rather than a table. Each event is a row: a continuous 1px vertical spine on the left that is verified-green while chain.verified holds; an 8-character mono hash prefix; the event type in uppercase mono; a tabular timestamp; and the actor (agent / human / system) as a small glyph. At broken_at_seq the spine breaks with a visible gap, the colour switches to the blocking red, and everything below is rendered at reduced emphasis. Break-glass overrides get the magenta 'commanded' colour and a wider row. The suppression ledger (M3) uses the same spine, with revocations drawn as a strikethrough row that remains present — never removed — because append-only is the whole point and the UI must make deletion look impossible. | WHY: Audit interfaces have four settled visual grammars — list, detail, timeline, aggregated — and a hash chain is inherently a progression, so timeline is the correct one; a table would throw away the single most valuable visual fact, which is that the chain is continuous. Drawing the break as a literal physical gap in a continuous line is the cheapest possible way to make 'tamper-evident' legible to a judge in two seconds. | TRADEOFF: Vertical timelines are less dense than tables and scroll longer; you will need virtualisation past a few hundred events and a compact/expanded toggle. The spine metaphor also constrains you to one event per row — no multi-column comparison.
  - (high) Apply a hard glass budget: backdrop-filter is permitted in exactly two places in the entire product — the command palette (cmdk 1.1.1) and the sticky header — and nowhere else. No frosted card over a photograph, anywhere, ever. Everywhere else use solid surfaces from the ladder plus the Linear treatments: 1px hairline (0.5px at >=2x DPR), the corner edge-highlight, and a 256x256 tiled grain at opacity .6 on marketing sections only. Use elevation-as-lightness (step up the surface ladder), never drop shadows, on any data surface. | WHY: Glassmorphism has been demoted to a supporting element for 2026 and its Dribbble form — frosted panels over a cinematic photo — is precisely the part that fails in product, because backdrop-filter over live scrolling data costs GPU every frame and destroys text contrast the instant real content moves underneath. Material's elevation-overlay model (0% white at 0dp to 16% at 24dp) is the correct dark-mode depth idiom and costs nothing. Linear's grain plus corner-specular is the cheapest 'expensive' available and does not touch the compositor on scroll. | TRADEOFF: Two glass surfaces is stingy and the dashboard will look flatter than the Dribbble reference set. Accept it: flatness plus immaculate hairlines is the 'forensic' register you want, and shadows on a dark data grid always read as muddy anyway.
  - (medium) Use the bento grid exactly once, for M1-M4, and make it asymmetric and active. Four tiles, not equal: M1 PROOF gets a double-width tile containing the live base/head terminal; M2 SEVERITY gets a tall tile with the contribution bar; M3 FINGERPRINT and M4 GOVERNANCE get standard tiles. On hover a tile does not merely tint — it plays its mechanic (the fingerprint tile re-renames a variable and shifts lines while the hash stays identical; the governance tile attempts an APPROVE and gets refused in code). Every other section of the page must break the grid rhythm. | WHY: Bento is at ~67% adoption among top-tier SaaS landing pages and has been the default for new B2B homepage launches since mid-2026, so a full-page bento now reads as template. But four mechanics is genuinely four-tile content, and 'active bento' (tile opens/plays on hover) is the 2026 evolution rather than the 2024 cliché. 925studios names the row-of-three-equal-rounded-cards reflex specifically; asymmetry is the stated fix. | TRADEOFF: Active tiles are a real build cost — four bespoke micro-demos — and they are the first thing to break on mobile, where they must degrade to a single column of autoplaying-on-scroll cards. Also risks stealing attention from the hero terminal; keep the tile demos silent and short (<3s loops).
  - (high) Set a strict motion contract and make the one pulsing element be real data. Durations 120ms (state), 240ms (element enter), 400ms (section reveal), 900ms max (pipeline stage walk). Easings lifted from Linear: --ease-out-quint cubic-bezier(.23,1,.32,1) for enters, --ease-out-expo cubic-bezier(.19,1,.22,1) for big reveals, --ease-in-out-quart cubic-bezier(.77,0,.175,1) for the pipeline traverse. Animate transform and opacity only. Wrap everything in prefers-reduced-motion and ship a genuinely static fallback (the pipeline renders fully complete). And: the only pulsing indicator in the product is the run heartbeat, pulsing at the ACTUAL heartbeat interval, with 'Stage 3/9 - specialists, 12s since last output' in mono beside it — if the heartbeat is stale the pulse stops and goes amber. | WHY: impeccable.style names 'a pulsing status dot draws attention even when nothing changes' as slop. You can convert the single most-clichéd AI UI element into the most on-brand one in the product by making its rate literally equal to real telemetry — that is the difference between decoration and instrumentation, which is the entire positioning. GSAP 3.15.0 with free ScrollTrigger makes the choreography cheap. | TRADEOFF: Tying the pulse to real heartbeat data means the demo looks broken if the backend stalls — which is arguably correct, but risky live on stage. Have a deterministic replay mode for the demo that still drives the pulse from recorded timings rather than a CSS loop.
  - (high) Tune the dashboard for density, not for screenshots: 13px base text (Linear's 'small'), 15px only for primary content; row height 28-32px in finding and audit lists; radii capped at 6px on data surfaces and 10-12px on containers (borrow Graphite's 2/6/8/12 rather than Linear's marketing 12/16/24/32); 1px hairlines instead of cards; no more than 5 accent colours on screen at once (the five proof states); all numerics tabular + slashed-zero. Ship a real keyboard layer: cmdk 1.1.1 palette, j/k list traversal, / to filter, s to suppress (which forces the reason field), g-a to the audit chain. And build the finding list on @tanstack/react-table 9.2.4 with virtualisation from day one, because the Dribbble reference set has no answer for 400 findings. | WHY: Muzli's 50-shot survey is explicit that these designs are built to win a thumbnail, not to survive daily use, and the standard critique is that they cannot represent real density, role permissions, live feeds or a data source going down. PRGuard's dashboard is a triage surface for a reviewer under time pressure; keyboard-fast and dense is both the stated requirement and the credible differentiator against a competitor whose product surface is a GitHub comment. | TRADEOFF: 13px base is below the 16px body floor that accessibility guidance recommends for reading content — it is defensible for a professional data tool but not for the marketing site, so the two properties will have different type scales despite sharing a brand. Density also means more of the design lives in states and empty/error cases, which is unglamorous work.
